Let me first edit parser.go. I'll use Python for precise replacements.

First, refactor parseBinaryExpr:
Current:
```go
func (p *Parser) parseBinaryExpr(prec1 int) Expr {
        ...
        x := p.parseUnaryExpr()

        for {
                op, prec := p.token, p.token.Precedence()
                if prec < prec1 {
                        return x
                }

                pos := p.expect(op)

                y := p.parseBinaryExpr(prec + 1)

                x = &BinaryExpr{
                        LHS:      x,
                        RHS:      y,
                        Token:    op,
                        TokenPos: pos,
                }
        }
}
```
Replace with:
```go
func (p *Parser) parseBinaryExpr(prec1 int) Expr {
        x := p.parseUnaryExpr()
        return p.parseBinaryExprTail(x, prec1)
}

func (p *Parser) parseBinaryExprTail(x Expr, prec1 int) Expr {
        for {
                op, prec := p.token, p.token.Precedence()
                if prec < prec1 {
                        return x
                }
                pos := p.expect(op)
                y := p.parseBinaryExpr(prec + 1)
                x = &BinaryExpr{LHS: x, RHS: y, Token: op, TokenPos: pos}
        }
}
```

Then modify `parseSimpleStmt`:
- `x := p.parseExprListPattern()`
- In `case token.Assign, token.Define`, before returning, if LHS contains pattern and tok == token.Assign, emit error "cannot use destructuring with
=". Actually we can let compiler do it. But parser is also fine. Let me do it in compiler to keep parser simpler. However, there's a case: `[a] = x` —
if we leave it to compiler, compiler will see pattern LHS and error. Good.
- For the `case token.In` handling, if x[0] is a pattern (not Ident), it already errors. Fine.
- The later switch handles `Define, +=, etc.` for single expr; if x[0] is a pattern, it'll create AssignStmt with pattern LHS and op Define (for
`:=`), or other ops. For `[a] += x`, compiler will error "cannot use destructuring with =" or some op not allowed. Actually `+=` with pattern should
error. We'll handle in compiler: if LHS is pattern and op != Define -> error "cannot use destructuring with =". The error substring requirement is
only for `=`, so for `+=` any message fine.

Now `parseExprListPattern`:
```go
func (p *Parser) parseExprListPattern() (list []Expr) {
        if p.trace { defer untracep(tracep(p, "PatternExpressionList")) }
        list = append(list, p.parseExprPattern())
        for p.token == token.Comma {
                p.next()
                list = append(list, p.parseExprPattern())
        }
        return
}
```

`parseExprPattern`:
```go
func (p *Parser) parseExprPattern() Expr {
        if p.token == token.LBrack || p.token == token.LBrace {
                pat := p.parsePattern()
                if p.token != token.Assign && p.token != token.Define {
                        lit := p.convertPatternToLiteral(pat)
                        if lit == nil {
                                return pat
                        }
                        return p.parseBinaryExprTail(lit, token.LowestPrec+1)
                }
                return pat
        }
        return p.parseExpr()
}
```

Wait, but what about a pattern used in an expression context like `x = [a,b] := ...`? No, `:=` only at statement start. And `f([a,b])` RHS uses
parseExpr (normal literal). Good.

But there's a subtle issue: `parseExprPattern` is called for each element in `parseExprListPattern`. If the first LHS is a pattern and there's a comma
(e.g., `[a], b := ...`), the second element `b` is parsed by `parseExprPattern` which calls `parseExpr()` — fine. Then compileAssign errors tuple
assignment. Good.

Now `parsePattern`:
```go
func (p *Parser) parsePattern() Expr {
        switch p.token {
        case token.LBrack:
                return p.parseArrayPattern()
        case token.LBrace:
                return p.parseMapPattern()
        }
        return p.parseExpr()
}
```

`parseArrayPattern`:
```go
func (p *Parser) parseArrayPattern() Expr {
        lbrack := p.expect(token.LBrack)
        var elements []Expr
        for p.token != token.RBrack && p.token != token.EOF {
                elem := p.parsePatternElement()
                elements = append(elements, elem)

                if _, isRest := elem.(*RestExpr); isRest {
                        // rest element must be the last element
                        if p.token == token.Comma {
                                p.next()
                                if p.token != token.RBrack {
                                        p.error(p.pos, "rest element must be last")
                                }
                        }
                        break
                }
                if !p.expectComma(token.RBrack, "array pattern element") {
                        break
                }
        }
        rbrack := p.expect(token.RBrack)
        return &ArrayPattern{LBrack: lbrack, Elements: elements, RBrack: rbrack}
}
```

Hmm, but if rest is parsed and then `,` followed by another element, we consume the comma, error, and break — leaving the next element unconsumed, so
`p.expect(RBrack)` will fail with "expected ']'" which is okay but might cascade errors. Better to continue consuming/advancing to recover. Let me
improve:
```go
if _, isRest := elem.(*RestExpr); isRest {
    if p.token == token.Comma {
        p.next()
        if p.token != token.RBrack {
            p.error(p.pos, "rest element must be last")
            // consume remaining elements for recovery
            for p.token != token.RBrack && p.token != token.EOF {
                p.parsePatternElement()
                if !p.expectComma(token.RBrack, "array pattern element") {
                    break
                }
            }
        }
    }
    break
}
```
That's more robust. Let me implement this.

`parsePatternElement`:
```go
func (p *Parser) parsePatternElement() Expr {
        if p.token == token.Ellipsis {
                ellipsisPos := p.expect(token.Ellipsis)
                expr := p.parseExpr()
                return &RestExpr{Expr: expr, EllipsisPos: ellipsisPos}
        }

        var x Expr
        switch p.token {
        case token.LBrack:
                x = p.parseArrayPattern()
        case token.LBrace:
                x = p.parseMapPattern()
        default:
                x = p.parseExpr()
        }

        if p.token == token.Assign {
                pos := p.expect(token.Assign)
                def := p.parseExpr()
                x = &DefaultExpr{Expr: x, Default: def, TokenPos: pos}
        }
        return x
}
```

Wait, but `parseExpr()` on `a` will parse the ident; then token `=`; we wrap. Good. For `[a, b = 50]`, element `b = 50` parsed as `b` then `=` then
`50`. Good.

But there's a problem: `parseExpr()` for a default expression like `a = [1,2]` — after `=`, `parseExpr()` parses `[1,2]` as an ArrayLit (normal
literal). Good.

`parseMapPattern`:
```go
func (p *Parser) parseMapPattern() Expr {
        lbrace := p.expect(token.LBrace)
        var fields []*MapPatternField
        for p.token != token.RBrace && p.token != token.EOF {
                fields = append(fields, p.parseMapPatternField())
                if !p.expectComma(token.RBrace, "map pattern element") {
                        break
                }
        }
        rbrace := p.expect(token.RBrace)
        return &MapPattern{LBrace: lbrace, Fields: fields, RBrace: rbrace}
}
```

`parseMapPatternField`:
```go
func (p *Parser) parseMapPatternField() *MapPatternField {
        keyPos := p.pos
        keyName := "_"
        if p.token == token.Ident {
                keyName = p.tokenLit
        } else if p.token == token.String {
                v, _ := strconv.Unquote(p.tokenLit)
                keyName = v
        } else {
                p.errorExpected(p.pos, "map pattern key")
        }
        p.next()

        if p.token == token.Colon {
                p.next()
                value := p.parsePatternElement()
                return &MapPatternField{Key: keyName, KeyPos: keyPos, Value: value}
        }

        if p.token == token.Assign {
                pos := p.expect(token.Assign)
                def := p.parseExpr()
                ident := &Ident{Name: keyName, NamePos: keyPos}
                return &MapPatternField{
                        Key:    keyName,
                        KeyPos: keyPos,
                        Value:  &DefaultExpr{Expr: ident, Default: def, TokenPos: pos},
                }
        }

        return &MapPatternField{Key: keyName, KeyPos: keyPos, Value: nil}
}
```

Wait, there's a problem with shorthand default `{x = 50}`: the field value is DefaultExpr{Ident x, 50}. But what if the key is a string `{"x" = 50}`?
That's weird but we could support; the Ident name would be keyName which is "x". Fine.

`convertPatternToLiteral`:
```go
func (p *Parser) convertPatternToLiteral(expr Expr) Expr {
        switch pat := expr.(type) {
        case *ArrayPattern:
                var elements []Expr
                for _, e := range pat.Elements {
                        lit := p.convertPatternToLiteral(e)
                        if lit == nil {
                                return nil
                        }
                        elements = append(elements, lit)
                }
                return &ArrayLit{LBrack: pat.LBrack, Elements: elements, RBrack: pat.RBrack}
        case *MapPattern:
                var elements []*MapElementLit
                for _, f := range pat.Fields {
                        if f.Value == nil {
                                p.error(f.KeyPos, "expected ':'")
                                return nil
                        }
                        lit := p.convertPatternToLiteral(f.Value)
                        if lit == nil {
                                return nil
                        }
                        elements = append(elements, &MapElementLit{
                                Key: f.Key, KeyPos: f.KeyPos, Value: lit,
                        })
                }
                return &MapLit{LBrace: pat.LBrace, Elements: elements, RBrace: pat.RBrace}
        case *DefaultExpr:
                p.error(pat.Pos(), "default value not allowed here")
                return nil
        case *RestExpr:
                p.error(pat.Pos(), "rest element not allowed here")
                return nil
        default:
                return pat
        }
}
```

Now, `parseIdentList` modification:
Current:
```go
func (p *Parser) parseIdentList() *IdentList {
        var params []*Ident
        lparen := p.expect(token.LParen)
        isVarArgs := false
        if p.token != token.RParen {
                if p.token == token.Ellipsis {
                        isVarArgs = true
                        p.next()
                }
                params = append(params, p.parseIdent())
                for !isVarArgs && p.token == token.Comma {
                        p.next()
                        if p.token == token.Ellipsis {
                                isVarArgs = true
                                p.next()
                        }
                        params = append(params, p.parseIdent())
                }
        }
        rparen := p.expect(token.RParen)
        return &IdentList{...}
}
```
New:
```go
func (p *Parser) parseIdentList() *IdentList {
        var params []Expr
        lparen := p.expect(token.LParen)
        isVarArgs := false
        if p.token != token.RParen {
                if p.token == token.Ellipsis {
                        isVarArgs = true
                        p.next()
                }
                params = append(params, p.parseParam())
                for !isVarArgs && p.token == token.Comma {
                        p.next()
                        if p.token == token.Ellipsis {
                                isVarArgs = true
                                p.next()
                        }
                        params = append(params, p.parseParam())
                }
        }
        rparen := p.expect(token.RParen)
        return &IdentList{
                LParen: lparen,
                RParen: rparen,
                VarArgs: isVarArgs,
                List: params,
        }
}

func (p *Parser) parseParam() Expr {
        var x Expr
        if p.token == token.LBrack || p.token == token.LBrace {
                x = p.parsePattern()
        } else {
                x = p.parseIdent()
        }
        if p.token == token.Assign {
                pos := p.expect(token.Assign)
                def := p.parseExpr()
                x = &DefaultExpr{Expr: x, Default: def, TokenPos: pos}
        }
        return x
}
```

Wait, but `parseIdent` returns `*Ident`. If a param is `a = 1`, `parseParam` wraps in DefaultExpr. Good. If a param is `[a] = [1]`, `parsePattern`
returns ArrayPattern, then wraps in DefaultExpr. Good.

But there's a problem: `parsePattern` when called in params for `[a]` will parse as ArrayPattern. If followed by `)` or `,`, returns pattern. Good.
But `parsePattern` is lenient and may parse `a + b` as `BinaryExpr` if the token after `[` is `a + b`. That's invalid in params; compiler will error.

Now, the compiler's `NumParameters = len(node.Type.Params.List)` counts each param including defaults/patterns as 1. Good.

Now, let's also consider that `parseSimpleStmt` currently starts with `x := p.parseExprList()`. I'll change to `parseExprListPattern()`. But wait,
there's a subtlety: for a simple `x := 1`, `parseExprPattern()` calls `parseExpr()` for ident x; fine. For `x[0] := 1`, parseExpr() parses IndexExpr;
then `:=`; AssignStmt LHS IndexExpr; compiler errors "operator ':=' not allowed with selector". Good.

Now, what about `[a,b] := x` when `parseExprPattern` parses `[a,b]` as ArrayPattern and sees `:=`, returns pattern. `parseExprListPattern` returns
[pattern]. Then `parseSimpleStmt` creates AssignStmt with pattern LHS. Good.

Now, let me also think about `parseExprPattern` when the pattern is followed by `,` in a list. E.g., `[a], b := ...`. `parseExprPattern` parses `[a]`
as pattern; `p.token` is `,` (not assign/define), so it converts to literal and calls `parseBinaryExprTail`. `parseBinaryExprTail` sees token `,` with
precedence 0 < LowestPrec+1, returns literal. Then `parseExprListPattern` sees comma, parses `b`. So LHS = [ArrayLit, Ident], and `:=` -> AssignStmt.
Compiler will error tuple assignment. But wait — for `[a], b := x`, should this be treated as two patterns? Not supported anyway. Error fine.

Now, let's make the edits.

func (p *Parser) parseBinaryExprTail(x Expr, prec1 int) Expr {
	for {
		op, prec := p.token, p.token.Precedence()
		if prec < prec1 {
			return x
		}

		pos := p.expect(op)

		y := p.parseBinaryExpr(prec + 1)

		x = &BinaryExpr{
			LHS:      x,

			Token:    op,
			TokenPos: pos,
		}
	}

addition=anchor+'''

// parseExprListPattern parses a list of expressions that may contain
// destructuring patterns on the left-hand side of an assignment.
func (p *Parser) parseExprListPattern() (list []Expr) {
	if p.trace {
		defer untracep(tracep(p, "PatternExpressionList"))
	}

	list = append(list, p.parseExprPattern())
	for p.token == token.Comma {
		p.next()
		list = append(list, p.parseExprPattern())
	}
	return
}

// parseExprPattern parses an expression that may be a destructuring pattern.
func (p *Parser) parseExprPattern() Expr {
	if p.token == token.LBrack || p.token == token.LBrace {
		pat := p.parsePattern()
		if p.token != token.Assign && p.token != token.Define {
			if lit := p.convertPatternToLiteral(pat); lit != nil {
				return p.parseBinaryExprTail(lit, token.LowestPrec+1)
			}
		}
		return pat
	}
	return p.parseExpr()
}

// parsePattern parses a destructuring pattern.
func (p *Parser) parsePattern() Expr {
	switch p.token {
	case token.LBrack:
		return p.parseArrayPattern()
	case token.LBrace:
		return p.parseMapPattern()
	}
	return p.parseExpr()
}

// parseArrayPattern parses an array pattern: [elem, ...].
func (p *Parser) parseArrayPattern() Expr {
	if p.trace {
		defer untracep(tracep(p, "ArrayPattern"))
	}

	lbrack := p.expect(token.LBrack)

	var elements []Expr
	for p.token != token.RBrack && p.token != token.EOF {
		elem := p.parsePatternElement()
		elements = append(elements, elem)

		if _, isRest := elem.(*RestExpr); isRest {
			// rest element must be the last element
			if p.token == token.Comma {
				p.next()
				if p.token != token.RBrack {
					p.error(p.pos, "rest element must be last")
					for p.token != token.RBrack && p.token != token.EOF {
						p.parsePatternElement()
						if !p.expectComma(token.RBrack,
							"array pattern element") {
							break
						}
					}
				}
			}
			break
		}

		if !p.expectComma(token.RBrack, "array pattern element") {
			break
		}
	}

	rbrack := p.expect(token.RBrack)
	return &ArrayPattern{
		LBrack:   lbrack,
		Elements: elements,
		RBrack:   rbrack,
	}
}

// parseMapPattern parses a map pattern: {key: value, ...}.
func (p *Parser) parseMapPattern() Expr {
	if p.trace {
		defer untracep(tracep(p, "MapPattern"))
	}

	lbrace := p.expect(token.LBrace)

	var fields []*MapPatternField
	for p.token != token.RBrace && p.token != token.EOF {
		fields = append(fields, p.parseMapPatternField())

		if !p.expectComma(token.RBrace, "map pattern element") {
			break
		}
	}

	rbrace := p.expect(token.RBrace)
	return &MapPattern{
		LBrace: lbrace,
		Fields: fields,
		RBrace: rbrace,
	}
}

// parseMapPatternField parses a single map pattern field.
func (p *Parser) parseMapPatternField() *MapPatternField {
	keyPos := p.pos
	keyName := "_"
	if p.token == token.Ident {
		keyName = p.tokenLit
	} else if p.token == token.String {
		v, _ := strconv.Unquote(p.tokenLit)
		keyName = v
	} else {
		p.errorExpected(keyPos, "map pattern key")
	}
	p.next()

	if p.token == token.Colon {
		p.next()
		value := p.parsePatternElement()
		return &MapPatternField{
			Key:    keyName,
			KeyPos: keyPos,
			Value:  value,
		}
	}

	if p.token == token.Assign {
		pos := p.expect(token.Assign)
		def := p.parseExpr()
		return &MapPatternField{
			Key:    keyName,
			KeyPos: keyPos,
			Value: &DefaultExpr{
				Expr:     &Ident{Name: keyName, NamePos: keyPos},
				Default:  def,
				TokenPos: pos,
			},
		}
	}

	return &MapPatternField{
		Key:    keyName,
		KeyPos: keyPos,
		Value:  nil,
	}
}

// parsePatternElement parses an element of an array pattern or the value of a
// map pattern.
func (p *Parser) parsePatternElement() Expr {
	if p.token == token.Ellipsis {
		ellipsisPos := p.expect(token.Ellipsis)
		expr := p.parseExpr()
		return &RestExpr{
			Expr:        expr,
			EllipsisPos: ellipsisPos,
		}
	}

	var x Expr
	switch p.token {
	case token.LBrack:
		x = p.parseArrayPattern()
	case token.LBrace:
		x = p.parseMapPattern()
	default:
		x = p.parseExpr()
	}

	if p.token == token.Assign {
		pos := p.expect(token.Assign)
		def := p.parseExpr()
		x = &DefaultExpr{
			Expr:     x,
			Default:  def,
			TokenPos: pos,
		}
	}
	return x
}

// convertPatternToLiteral converts a pattern AST back into a regular
// expression AST when it is not used as a destructuring target.
func (p *Parser) convertPatternToLiteral(expr Expr) Expr {
	switch pat := expr.(type) {
	case *ArrayPattern:
		var elements []Expr
		for _, e := range pat.Elements {
			lit := p.convertPatternToLiteral(e)
			if lit == nil {
				return nil
			}
			elements = append(elements, lit)
		}
		return &ArrayLit{
			LBrack:   pat.LBrack,
			Elements: elements,
			RBrack:   pat.RBrack,
		}
	case *MapPattern:
		var elements []*MapElementLit
		for _, f := range pat.Fields {
			if f.Value == nil {
				p.error(f.KeyPos, "expected ':'")
				return nil
			}
			lit := p.convertPatternToLiteral(f.Value)
			if lit == nil {
				return nil
			}
			elements = append(elements, &MapElementLit{
				Key:    f.Key,
				KeyPos: f.KeyPos,
				Value:  lit,
			})
		}
		return &MapLit{
			LBrace:   pat.LBrace,
			Elements: elements,
			RBrace:   pat.RBrace,
		}
	case *DefaultExpr:
		p.error(pat.Pos(), "default value not allowed here")
		return nil
	case *RestExpr:
		p.error(pat.Pos(), "rest element not allowed here")
		return nil
	default:
		return pat
	}
}
'''
assert anchor in s
s=s.replace(anchor, addition)
open(p,'w').write(s)
print("ok")

					//

Now let's implement compiler changes. I'll create a new file `destructure.go` in the root package for the destructuring compilation logic, keeping
compiler.go cleaner.

First, let's write helper methods. We need access to `c.errorf`, `c.emit`, `c.addConstant`, `c.Compile`, `c.symbolTable`. All in package tengo.

Let me design `compilePattern` carefully.

```go
package tengo

import (
        "fmt"
        "github.com/d5/tengo/v2/parser"
        "github.com/d5/tengo/v2/token"
)

// definePatternSymbol defines a new symbol for a pattern binding.
func (c *Compiler) definePatternSymbol(node parser.Node, name string) (*Symbol, error) {
        symbol, depth, exists := c.symbolTable.Resolve(name, false)
        if depth == 0 && exists {
                return nil, c.errorf(node, "'%s' redeclared in this block", name)
        }
        return c.symbolTable.Define(name), nil
}

// storeSymbol stores the value on top of the stack into a symbol.
func (c *Compiler) storeSymbol(node parser.Node, sym *Symbol) {
        switch sym.Scope {
        case ScopeGlobal:
                c.emit(node, parser.OpSetGlobal, sym.Index)
        case ScopeLocal:
                sym.LocalAssigned = true
                c.emit(node, parser.OpDefineLocal, sym.Index)
        case ScopeFree:
                c.emit(node, parser.OpSetFree, sym.Index)
        default:
                panic(fmt.Errorf("invalid pattern symbol scope: %s", sym.Scope))
        }
}

// loadSymbol pushes the value of a symbol onto the stack.
func (c *Compiler) loadSymbol(node parser.Node, sym *Symbol) {
        switch sym.Scope {
        case ScopeGlobal:
                c.emit(node, parser.OpGetGlobal, sym.Index)
        case ScopeLocal:
                c.emit(node, parser.OpGetLocal, sym.Index)
        case ScopeFree:
                c.emit(node, parser.OpGetFree, sym.Index)
        default:
                panic(fmt.Errorf("invalid pattern symbol scope: %s", sym.Scope))
        }
}
```

Wait, `storeSymbol` for ScopeFree: pattern bindings in a function that capture free vars? That shouldn't happen because destructuring defines new
locals in the current scope. But a nested function could capture them. The symbol being defined here is always in current scope, so ScopeGlobal or
ScopeLocal. Free only if a closure captures a local... but define happens before capture. So panic on free is fine; we can handle it anyway with
OpSetFree but OpSetFree semantics: `OpSetFree` stores to free var index. If a pattern leaf somehow resolves to a free symbol? No, `Define` always
creates new local/global. So no.

Actually, for `compilePatternBindings`, the source symbol may be a free var? In destructuring assignment inside a function, the source is a local temp
(newly defined) or a global. In function params, source is a local temp. So loadSymbol for local/global only. Fine.

Now `compileDestructuringAssign`:
```go
func (c *Compiler) compileDestructuringAssign(node parser.Node, pattern parser.Expr, rhs parser.Expr) error {
        if err := c.Compile(rhs); err != nil {
                return err
        }
        source := c.symbolTable.Define(":destructure")
        c.storeSymbol(node, source)
        return c.compilePattern(node, pattern, source)
}
```

Wait, but for a simple Ident LHS like `a := 1`, `isDestructuringPattern` returns false, so we don't call this. Only patterns. Good.

Now `compilePattern`:
```go
func (c *Compiler) compilePattern(node parser.Node, pattern parser.Expr, source *Symbol) error {
        switch pat := pattern.(type) {
        case *parser.ArrayPattern:
                return c.compileArrayPattern(node, pat, source)
        case *parser.MapPattern:
                return c.compileMapPattern(node, pat, source)
        case *parser.Ident:
                return c.compileIdentPattern(node, pat, source)
        case *parser.DefaultExpr:
                return c.compileDefaultPattern(node, pat, source)
        case *parser.RestExpr:
                return c.compileRestPattern(node, pat, source)
        default:
                return c.errorf(node, "invalid destructuring pattern")
        }
}
```

`compileIdentPattern` for a leaf with source:
```go
func (c *Compiler) compileIdentPattern(node parser.Node, ident *parser.Ident, source *Symbol) error {
        c.loadSymbol(node, source)
        // bind whole source to ident? Actually for array element we need index.
}
```
Hmm, `compilePattern` is called with a source symbol that holds the parent value. For a leaf ident at the top level (only in params default case?
Actually in array/map we pass source=parent and need to extract element before binding leaf). So `compileIdentPattern` as a top-level pattern (when
source is the value to bind directly) is only used when a param is a simple Ident (but we don't call compilePattern for simple params) or when a
pattern element is an Ident extracted already.

Let me restructure: `compileArrayPattern` and `compileMapPattern` iterate elements/fields and for each element call `compilePatternElement(node, elem,
source, indexExpr)` which:
- loads source, pushes index, OpIndex -> value
- then binds the value to elem (leaf or nested).

For nested pattern, store value in a new temp and recurse.

For leaf ident, define symbol and store.

For default, apply default logic then bind inner pattern to value.

For rest, slice and bind.

So:

```go
func (c *Compiler) compileArrayPattern(node parser.Node, pat *parser.ArrayPattern, source *Symbol) error {
        for idx, elem := range pat.Elements {
                if err := c.compileArrayElement(node, elem, source, idx); err != nil {
                        return err
                }
        }
        return nil
}

func (c *Compiler) compileArrayElement(node parser.Node, elem parser.Expr, source *Symbol, idx int) error {
        indexExpr := &parser.IntLit{Value: int64(idx)}
        return c.compileIndexedPattern(node, elem, source, indexExpr)
}

func (c *Compiler) compileMapPattern(node parser.Node, pat *parser.MapPattern, source *Symbol) error {
        for _, field := range pat.Fields {
                if field.Value == nil {
                        // shorthand {x} -> bind key to same name
                        field = &parser.MapPatternField{
                                Key: field.Key,
                                KeyPos: field.KeyPos,
                                Value: &parser.Ident{Name: field.Key, NamePos: field.KeyPos},
                        }
                }
                keyExpr := &parser.StringLit{Value: field.Key}
                if err := c.compileIndexedPattern(node, field.Value, source, keyExpr); err != nil {
                        return err
                }
        }
        return nil
}
```

Wait, for map shorthand `{x}`, the value is `Ident{Name:x}`. But if there's a default `{x = 50}`, field.Value is DefaultExpr{Ident x, 50}, so
compileIndexedPattern handles default then binds ident.

`compileIndexedPattern`:
```go
func (c *Compiler) compileIndexedPattern(node parser.Node, elem parser.Expr, source *Symbol, index parser.Expr) error {
        c.loadSymbol(node, source)
        if err := c.Compile(index); err != nil {
                return err
        }
        c.emit(node, parser.OpIndex)
        return c.compilePatternValue(node, elem)
}
```

`compilePatternValue` binds the value on top of stack to elem:
```go
func (c *Compiler) compilePatternValue(node parser.Node, elem parser.Expr) error {
        switch pat := elem.(type) {
        case *parser.Ident:
                sym, err := c.definePatternSymbol(node, pat.Name)
                if err != nil { return err }
                c.storeSymbol(node, sym)
                return nil
        case *parser.ArrayPattern, *parser.MapPattern:
                // store value into a temp and recurse
                temp := c.symbolTable.Define(":destructure")
                c.storeSymbol(node, temp)
                return c.compilePattern(node, pat, temp)
        case *parser.DefaultExpr:
                return c.compileDefaultPattern(node, pat)
        case *parser.RestExpr:
                return c.compileRestPattern(node, pat)
        default:
                return c.errorf(node, "invalid destructuring pattern element")
        }
}
```

Wait, for `DefaultExpr`, after OpIndex the value is on stack. `compileDefaultPattern`:
```go
func (c *Compiler) compileDefaultPattern(node parser.Node, pat *parser.DefaultExpr) error {
        // stack: [value]
        jumpDefault := c.emit(node, parser.OpJumpIfUndefined, 0)
        // value is not undefined, keep it
        jumpEnd := c.emit(node, parser.OpJump, 0)
        // default label
        c.changeOperand(jumpDefault, len(c.currentInstructions()))
        if err := c.Compile(pat.Default); err != nil {
                return err
        }
        // end label
        c.changeOperand(jumpEnd, len(c.currentInstructions()))
        // stack: [value or default]
        return c.compilePatternValue(node, pat.Expr)
}
```

But wait, if `pat.Expr` is itself a nested pattern (e.g., `[a] = [1]`), `compilePatternValue` handles it. Good. If `pat.Expr` is Ident, binds. If it's
a nested array pattern, stores value to temp and recurses.

But there's an issue: `OpJumpIfUndefined` on not-jump leaves the value on stack. `OpJump` then jumps to end without popping. So after the jump chain,
the value (or default) is on stack. Then `compilePatternValue` consumes it. Good.

However, if `pat.Expr` is an ArrayPattern/MapPattern, `compilePatternValue` stores the value into a temp via `storeSymbol`, which pops it. Good.

Now `compileRestPattern` for array rest:
```go
func (c *Compiler) compileRestPattern(node parser.Node, pat *parser.RestExpr, source *Symbol, idx int) error {
        // We need the start index. compileRestPattern is called from compileArrayPattern
}
```
Let me restructure so `compileArrayElement` handles rest specially:
```go
func (c *Compiler) compileArrayElement(node parser.Node, elem parser.Expr, source *Symbol, idx int) error {
        if rest, ok := elem.(*parser.RestExpr); ok {
                // slice from idx to end
                c.loadSymbol(node, source)
                indexExpr := &parser.IntLit{Value: int64(idx)}
                if err := c.Compile(indexExpr); err != nil { return err }
                c.emit(node, parser.OpNull)  // high = undefined
                c.emit(node, parser.OpSliceIndex)
                // rest target must be an ident
                ident, ok := rest.Expr.(*parser.Ident)
                if !ok {
                        return c.errorf(node, "invalid rest pattern")
                }
                sym, err := c.definePatternSymbol(node, ident.Name)
                if err != nil { return err }
                c.storeSymbol(node, sym)
                return nil
        }
        // normal element
        ...
}
```

Wait, but the parser should already enforce rest is last. And rest target should be Ident (parser uses parseExpr which could be a nested pattern or
expression). We'll validate here.

For function param rest? `func(...rest)` is handled by VarArgs, not RestExpr. RestExpr only appears inside array patterns. So `compileRestPattern` as
a standalone top-level pattern isn't needed.

Now, `compilePattern` top-level: It's called with source symbol holding a value (for assignment, the RHS stored in temp; for params, the arg temp).
For a top-level ArrayPattern/MapPattern, we iterate and extract. For a top-level DefaultExpr (param `[a] = [1]`), we need to check if the source value
is undefined, apply default, store back into source temp, then recurse on inner pattern.

So `compilePattern` for DefaultExpr:
```go
case *parser.DefaultExpr:
    // stack manipulation on source
    c.loadSymbol(node, source)
    jumpDefault := c.emit(node, parser.OpJumpIfUndefined, 0)
    jumpEnd := c.emit(node, parser.OpJump, 0)
    c.changeOperand(jumpDefault, len(c.currentInstructions()))
    if err := c.Compile(pat.Default); err != nil { return err }
    c.changeOperand(jumpEnd, len(c.currentInstructions()))
    c.storeSymbol(node, source)  // replace source with value/default
    return c.compilePattern(node, pat.Expr, source)
```

Hmm but wait: at the `jumpEnd` label, if not undefined, we have the source value on stack (from loadSymbol). If undefined, we have the default. Then
storeSymbol stores whichever into source temp. Then recurse. Good.

But there's a subtlety: `OpJumpIfUndefined` pops the value on jump. On not-jump, value stays. Then `OpJump` goes to end. At end, value is on stack.
Good.

Now, `compilePattern` top-level for Ident isn't needed (simple params handled separately). But could be called if someone writes `a := b`? No, that's
not a pattern. Only if a DefaultExpr's inner is Ident and compilePattern is called recursively after storing default into source... Actually for
`compilePattern(DefaultExpr)` we recurse into `pat.Expr` which could be Ident (e.g., param `a = 1`). We can handle Ident in compilePattern:
```go
case *parser.Ident:
    sym, err := c.definePatternSymbol(node, pat.Name)
    if err != nil { return err }
    // bind source value to sym
    c.loadSymbol(node, source)
    c.storeSymbol(node, sym)
    return nil
```
But for params with default `a = 1`, we don't use this path; we handle directly (define a as param, then prologue). Actually we could reuse
compilePattern: define temp for param, compilePattern with DefaultExpr... Hmm, let me think.

Simpler: For all params (including simple), define temp symbols (even for simple idents? No—simple idents should occupy their arg slot directly,
because NumParameters and arg count mapping depends on it).

Let me define param handling:

For each param `p` at index i:
- If `p` is `*parser.Ident`: define symbol `p.Name`, LocalAssigned=true. This is the arg slot.
- If `p` is `*parser.DefaultExpr` and inner is `*parser.Ident`: define symbol `inner.Name`, LocalAssigned=true. This is the arg slot. The default
prologue will check and maybe replace.
- If `p` is `*parser.DefaultExpr` and inner is a pattern: define temp `:param{i}`, LocalAssigned=true. Prologue applies default to temp then
destructures.
- If `p` is `*parser.ArrayPattern` or `*parser.MapPattern`: define temp `:param{i}`, LocalAssigned=true.
- If `p` is `*parser.RestExpr`: error (top-level rest handled by VarArgs).
- Else error.

Then prologue:
- For each param i:
  - If `p` is `*parser.DefaultExpr` and inner is Ident: apply default to the ident's symbol (local). Sequence:
    ```
    load ident symbol
    OpJumpIfUndefined defaultLabel
    OpJump end
    defaultLabel:
      compile default
    end:
      store ident symbol
    ```
  - If `p` is DefaultExpr with pattern inner: load temp, apply default, store temp, then compilePattern(inner, temp).
  - If `p` is ArrayPattern/MapPattern: compilePattern(p, temp).

But wait, for `DefaultExpr` with inner Ident, after applying default we store into the same symbol (the arg slot). Since the arg is already at that
slot, if not undefined we load it and store it back (redundant but harmless). Could optimize but fine.

Now, one important consideration: `definePatternSymbol` in function params would check redeclaration. Function params don't have redeclaration issues
(each param name unique). But we should still error if duplicate param names? Currently `func(a, a)` — the second `Define("a")` overwrites the first
symbol in the store, and both params map to same local slot? Actually `Define` increments numDefinition, so second a gets next index. But `store["a"]`
is overwritten with the second symbol. So `a` refers to second param, first param inaccessible. Existing behavior allows it silently. Not our concern.

For destructuring params, duplicate leaf names should probably error, but not required. `Define` will overwrite. Fine.

Now, the key issue: `compilePattern` for array/map in params uses `definePatternSymbol` which checks redeclaration. Since leaf names are defined after
param symbols, they won't collide with param names unless same name as a param. E.g., `func([a], a)` — leaf a and param a would collide;
`definePatternSymbol` would error "redeclared". Fine.

Now, for `compilePattern` in params, leaves are defined in function scope. After prologue, `compilePattern` emits OpDefineLocal for each leaf. Good.

Now, `NumLocals = c.symbolTable.MaxSymbols()` after all definitions. Good.

Let me also think about the `storeSymbol` for leaf in a function: it's ScopeLocal, emits OpDefineLocal. It sets `sym.LocalAssigned = true`. Good, so
closures/free vars capture correctly.

Now, the `compileDestructuringAssign` top-level uses `:destructure` temp. But if destructuring occurs inside a function, `:destructure` is a local.
Good. If multiple nested patterns, we create additional temps with the same name `:destructure` — `Define(":destructure")` again would overwrite the
symbol in the store (same name)! That's a bug: `Define` for the same name in the same scope overwrites, and `numDefinition` increments, so the second
`:destructure` gets a new index, but `store[":destructure"]` points to the new one. However, the first temp symbol object is still referenced by our
local variable `source` in the recursion. Wait, in `compilePatternValue`, for nested pattern we do `temp := c.symbolTable.Define(":destructure")` and
then `compilePattern(node, pat, temp)`. The `temp` symbol object is the new one. The old source symbol object is still held by the caller's variable.
So even though the store overwrites the name, our symbol pointers remain valid and have correct indices. So reusing the same name is actually fine for
symbol objects! But there's a subtlety: `Define` increments `numDefinition`, so each nested temp gets a unique index, and `MaxSymbols` counts them.
Good.

But what about `symbolTable.Resolve` later if user code references `:destructure`? Not possible (invalid identifier). So no issue.

Actually, there's another subtlety: `Define(":destructure")` for a nested temp inside the same scope — `numDefinition` increments, so the next leaf
defined gets a higher index. That's fine; it just reserves a slot for the temp, which is initialized by `storeSymbol` (OpDefineLocal) when we store
the nested value. But the temp slot is defined/assigned, so no problem.

Wait, but `storeSymbol` for the nested temp emits OpDefineLocal which sets `LocalAssigned=true`. Then later when we `loadSymbol` it, Resolve isn't
used (we use the symbol object directly), so fine.

Now, another important issue: `definePatternSymbol` uses `Resolve(name, false)` to check redeclaration. For a leaf in a function's prologue, if the
leaf name equals a temp name? No. If leaf name equals a previously defined leaf (duplicate), Resolve would find it (LocalAssigned=true) and error.
Good.

But wait, for top-level destructuring in a block (local scope), `definePatternSymbol` calls `Resolve(name, false)`. If the name was defined in a
parent scope (e.g., global), `Resolve` returns depth > 0, so no redeclaration error — but `:=` inside a block shadows outer variables (that's allowed
in Go-style scoping). Then `Define` creates a new local symbol in the block. Good, consistent with existing `x := 1` inside `if` block when x is
global.

Now, let's also handle the "cannot use destructuring with =" error in compileAssign.

Modify compileAssign:
```go
func (c *Compiler) compileAssign(node parser.Node, lhs, rhs []parser.Expr, op token.Token) error {
        if len(lhs) == 1 && parser.IsPattern(lhs[0]) {
                if op != token.Define {
                        return c.errorf(node, "cannot use destructuring with =")
                }
                return c.compileDestructuringAssign(node, lhs[0], rhs[0])
        }
        ...
}
```
But if len(lhs) > 1 and one is a pattern, the existing tuple error would occur. We could keep that. E.g., `[a], b := x` -> tuple assignment error (not
pattern error). Fine.

What about `[a] += x`? op != Define -> "cannot use destructuring with =" — the message says `=` though op is `+=`. The requirement only needs the
substring for `=` errors. For `+=`, using the same message is acceptable (still a compile error). But maybe better: "cannot use destructuring with =".
It's fine.

package tengo

import (
	"fmt"

	"github.com/d5/tengo/v2/parser"
)

// definePatternSymbol defines a new symbol for a destructuring binding.
func (c *Compiler) definePatternSymbol(
	node parser.Node,
	name string,
) (*Symbol, error) {
	symbol, depth, exists := c.symbolTable.Resolve(name, false)
	if depth == 0 && exists {
		return nil, c.errorf(node, "'%s' redeclared in this block", name)
	}
	return c.symbolTable.Define(name), nil
}

// storeSymbol stores the value on top of the stack into a symbol.
func (c *Compiler) storeSymbol(node parser.Node, sym *Symbol) {
	switch sym.Scope {
	case ScopeGlobal:
		c.emit(node, parser.OpSetGlobal, sym.Index)
	case ScopeLocal:
		sym.LocalAssigned = true
		c.emit(node, parser.OpDefineLocal, sym.Index)
	case ScopeFree:
		c.emit(node, parser.OpSetFree, sym.Index)
	default:
		panic(fmt.Errorf("invalid pattern symbol scope: %s", sym.Scope))
	}
}

// loadSymbol pushes the value of a symbol onto the stack.
func (c *Compiler) loadSymbol(node parser.Node, sym *Symbol) {
	switch sym.Scope {
	case ScopeGlobal:
		c.emit(node, parser.OpGetGlobal, sym.Index)
	case ScopeLocal:
		c.emit(node, parser.OpGetLocal, sym.Index)
	case ScopeFree:
		c.emit(node, parser.OpGetFree, sym.Index)
	default:
		panic(fmt.Errorf("invalid pattern symbol scope: %s", sym.Scope))
	}
}

// compileDestructuringAssign compiles a destructuring assignment such as
// [a, b] := source or {x: a} := source.
func (c *Compiler) compileDestructuringAssign(
	node parser.Node,
	pattern parser.Expr,
	rhs parser.Expr,
) error {
	if err := c.Compile(rhs); err != nil {
		return err
	}
	source := c.symbolTable.Define(":destructure")
	c.storeSymbol(node, source)
	return c.compilePattern(node, pattern, source)
}

// compilePattern compiles bindings for the given pattern, reading values from
// the source symbol.
func (c *Compiler) compilePattern(
	node parser.Node,
	pattern parser.Expr,
	source *Symbol,
) error {
	switch pat := pattern.(type) {
	case *parser.ArrayPattern:
		return c.compileArrayPattern(node, pat, source)
	case *parser.MapPattern:
		return c.compileMapPattern(node, pat, source)
	case *parser.DefaultExpr:
		// apply the default value to the source then continue with the inner
		// pattern.
		c.loadSymbol(node, source)
		jumpDefault := c.emit(node, parser.OpJumpIfUndefined, 0)
		jumpEnd := c.emit(node, parser.OpJump, 0)
		c.changeOperand(jumpDefault, len(c.currentInstructions()))
		if err := c.Compile(pat.Default); err != nil {
			return err
		}
		c.changeOperand(jumpEnd, len(c.currentInstructions()))
		c.storeSymbol(node, source)
		return c.compilePattern(node, pat.Expr, source)
	case *parser.Ident:
		sym, err := c.definePatternSymbol(node, pat.Name)
		if err != nil {
			return err
		}
		c.loadSymbol(node, source)
		c.storeSymbol(node, sym)
		return nil
	case *parser.RestExpr:
		return c.errorf(node, "rest element must be last")
	default:
		return c.errorf(node, "invalid destructuring pattern")
	}
}

// compileArrayPattern compiles an array pattern.
func (c *Compiler) compileArrayPattern(
	node parser.Node,
	pat *parser.ArrayPattern,
	source *Symbol,
) error {
	for idx, elem := range pat.Elements {
		if err := c.compileArrayElement(node, elem, source, idx); err != nil {
			return err
		}
	}
	return nil
}

// compileArrayElement compiles a single element of an array pattern.
func (c *Compiler) compileArrayElement(
	node parser.Node,
	elem parser.Expr,
	source *Symbol,
	idx int,
) error {
	if rest, ok := elem.(*parser.RestExpr); ok {
		// rest element collects the remaining elements: source[idx:]
		c.loadSymbol(node, source)
		c.emit(node, parser.OpConstant, c.addConstant(&Int{Value: int64(idx)}))
		c.emit(node, parser.OpNull) // high bound (undefined)
		c.emit(node, parser.OpSliceIndex)

		ident, ok := rest.Expr.(*parser.Ident)
		if !ok {
			return c.errorf(node, "rest element must be an identifier")
		}
		sym, err := c.definePatternSymbol(node, ident.Name)
		if err != nil {
			return err
		}
		c.storeSymbol(node, sym)
		return nil
	}

	c.loadSymbol(node, source)
	if err := c.Compile(&parser.IntLit{Value: int64(idx)}); err != nil {
		return err
	}
	c.emit(node, parser.OpIndex)
	return c.compilePatternValue(node, elem)
}

// compileMapPattern compiles a map pattern.
func (c *Compiler) compileMapPattern(
	node parser.Node,
	pat *parser.MapPattern,
	source *Symbol,
) error {
	for _, field := range pat.Fields {
		value := field.Value
		if value == nil {
			// shorthand {x} binds the key to a same-named variable.
			value = &parser.Ident{Name: field.Key, NamePos: field.KeyPos}
		}
		c.loadSymbol(node, source)
		c.emit(node, parser.OpConstant, c.addConstant(&String{Value: field.Key}))
		c.emit(node, parser.OpIndex)
		if err := c.compilePatternValue(node, value); err != nil {
			return err
		}
	}
	return nil
}

// compilePatternValue binds the value currently on top of the stack to the
// given pattern element.
func (c *Compiler) compilePatternValue(
	node parser.Node,
	elem parser.Expr,
) error {
	switch pat := elem.(type) {
	case *parser.Ident:
		sym, err := c.definePatternSymbol(node, pat.Name)
		if err != nil {
			return err
		}
		c.storeSymbol(node, sym)
		return nil
	case *parser.ArrayPattern, *parser.MapPattern:
		// store the sub-value into a temporary and compile the nested pattern.
		temp := c.symbolTable.Define(":destructure")
		c.storeSymbol(node, temp)
		return c.compilePattern(node, pat, temp)
	case *parser.DefaultExpr:
		return c.compileDefaultPattern(node, pat)
	case *parser.RestExpr:
		return c.errorf(node, "rest element must be last")
	default:
		return c.errorf(node, "invalid destructuring pattern element")
	}
}

// compileDefaultPattern compiles a pattern element with a default value. The
// value is on top of the stack; if it is undefined the default expression is
// evaluated instead.
func (c *Compiler) compileDefaultPattern(
	node parser.Node,
	pat *parser.DefaultExpr,
) error {
	jumpDefault := c.emit(node, parser.OpJumpIfUndefined, 0)
	jumpEnd := c.emit(node, parser.OpJump, 0)
	c.changeOperand(jumpDefault, len(c.currentInstructions()))
	if err := c.Compile(pat.Default); err != nil {
		return err
	}
	c.changeOperand(jumpEnd, len(c.currentInstructions()))
	return c.compilePatternValue(node, pat.Expr)
}

) error {

	if len(lhs) == 1 && parser.IsPattern(lhs[0]) {

			return c.errorf(node, "cannot use destructuring with =")
		}
		return c.compileDestructuringAssign(node, lhs[0], rhs[0])
	}

Actually, the param definition must happen in the function scope, before compiling prologue. Let me write helper:

```go
func (c *Compiler) compileFuncParams(node parser.Node, params []parser.Expr) error {
        // define parameter symbols
        for i, p := range params {
                switch param := p.(type) {
                case *parser.Ident:
                        s := c.symbolTable.Define(param.Name)
                        s.LocalAssigned = true
                case *parser.DefaultExpr:
                        if ident, ok := param.Expr.(*parser.Ident); ok {
                                s := c.symbolTable.Define(ident.Name)
                                s.LocalAssigned = true
                        } else if parser.IsPattern(param.Expr) {
                                s := c.symbolTable.Define(paramSymbolName(i))
                                s.LocalAssigned = true
                        } else {
                                return c.errorf(node, "invalid parameter")
                        }
                case *parser.ArrayPattern, *parser.MapPattern:
                        s := c.symbolTable.Define(paramSymbolName(i))
                        s.LocalAssigned = true
                default:
                        return c.errorf(node, "invalid parameter")
                }
        }

        // compile prologue for default/destructuring params
        for i, p := range params {
                switch param := p.(type) {
                case *parser.DefaultExpr:
                        if ident, ok := param.Expr.(*parser.Ident); ok {
                                sym, _, ok := c.symbolTable.Resolve(ident.Name, true)
                                if !ok {
                                        return c.errorf(node, "unresolved parameter '%s'", ident.Name)
                                }
                                if err := c.compileDefaultParam(node, param.Default, sym); err != nil {
                                        return err
                                }
                        } else {
                                sym, _, ok := c.symbolTable.Resolve(paramSymbolName(i), true)
                                if !ok {
                                        return c.errorf(node, "unresolved parameter")
                                }
                                // apply default to source
                                c.loadSymbol(node, sym)
                                jumpDefault := c.emit(node, parser.OpJumpIfUndefined, 0)
                                jumpEnd := c.emit(node, parser.OpJump, 0)
                                c.changeOperand(jumpDefault, len(c.currentInstructions()))
                                if err := c.Compile(param.Default); err != nil {
                                        return err
                                }
                                c.changeOperand(jumpEnd, len(c.currentInstructions()))
                                c.storeSymbol(node, sym)
                                if err := c.compilePattern(node, param.Expr, sym); err != nil {
                                        return err
                                }
                        }
                case *parser.ArrayPattern, *parser.MapPattern:
                        sym, _, ok := c.symbolTable.Resolve(paramSymbolName(i), true)
                        if !ok {
                                return c.errorf(node, "unresolved parameter")
                        }
                        if err := c.compilePattern(node, param, sym); err != nil {
                                return err
                        }
                }
        }
        return nil
}
```

func (c *Compiler) compileDefaultParam(node parser.Node, def parser.Expr, sym *Symbol) error {

        if err := c.Compile(def); err != nil {

func paramSymbolName(idx int) string {
	return fmt.Sprintf(":param%d", idx)
}

// compileFuncParams defines function parameter symbols and emits the prologue
// instructions needed for default values and destructuring parameters.
func (c *Compiler) compileFuncParams(
	node parser.Node,
	params []parser.Expr,
) error {
	// define parameter symbols
	for i, p := range params {
		switch param := p.(type) {
		case *parser.Ident:
			s := c.symbolTable.Define(param.Name)
			s.LocalAssigned = true
		case *parser.DefaultExpr:
			if ident, ok := param.Expr.(*parser.Ident); ok {
				s := c.symbolTable.Define(ident.Name)
				s.LocalAssigned = true
			} else if parser.IsPattern(param.Expr) {
				s := c.symbolTable.Define(paramSymbolName(i))
				s.LocalAssigned = true
			} else {
				return c.errorf(node, "invalid parameter")
			}
		case *parser.ArrayPattern, *parser.MapPattern:
			s := c.symbolTable.Define(paramSymbolName(i))
			s.LocalAssigned = true
		default:
			return c.errorf(node, "invalid parameter")
		}
	}

	// compile prologue instructions
	for i, p := range params {
		switch param := p.(type) {
		case *parser.DefaultExpr:
			if ident, ok := param.Expr.(*parser.Ident); ok {
				sym, _, ok := c.symbolTable.Resolve(ident.Name, true)
				if !ok {
					return c.errorf(node,
						"unresolved parameter '%s'", ident.Name)
				}
				if err := c.compileDefaultParam(node, param.Default, sym); err != nil {
					return err
				}
			} else {
				sym, _, ok := c.symbolTable.Resolve(paramSymbolName(i), true)
				if !ok {
					return c.errorf(node, "unresolved parameter")
				}
				c.loadSymbol(node, sym)
				jumpDefault := c.emit(node, parser.OpJumpIfUndefined, 0)
				jumpEnd := c.emit(node, parser.OpJump, 0)
				c.changeOperand(jumpDefault, len(c.currentInstructions()))
				if err := c.Compile(param.Default); err != nil {
					return err
				}
				c.changeOperand(jumpEnd, len(c.currentInstructions()))
				c.storeSymbol(node, sym)
				if err := c.compilePattern(node, param.Expr, sym); err != nil {
					return err
				}
			}
		case *parser.ArrayPattern, *parser.MapPattern:
			sym, _, ok := c.symbolTable.Resolve(paramSymbolName(i), true)
			if !ok {
				return c.errorf(node, "unresolved parameter")
			}
			if err := c.compilePattern(node, param, sym); err != nil {
				return err
			}
		}
	}
	return nil
}

// compileDefaultParam emits code that replaces an undefined function argument
// with its default value.
func (c *Compiler) compileDefaultParam(
	node parser.Node,
	def parser.Expr,
	sym *Symbol,
) error {
	c.loadSymbol(node, sym)
	jumpDefault := c.emit(node, parser.OpJumpIfUndefined, 0)
	jumpEnd := c.emit(node, parser.OpJump, 0)
	c.changeOperand(jumpDefault, len(c.currentInstructions()))
	if err := c.Compile(def); err != nil {
		return err
	}
	c.changeOperand(jumpEnd, len(c.currentInstructions()))
	c.storeSymbol(node, sym)
	return nil
}
EOF

func TestSliceIndex(t *testing.T) {
	expectError(t, `undefined[:1]`, nil, "Runtime Error: not indexable")
	expectError(t, `123[-1:2]`, nil, "Runtime Error: not indexable")
	expectError(t, `{}[:]`, nil, "Runtime Error: not indexable")
	expectError(t, `a := 123[-1:2] ; a += 1`, nil, "Runtime Error: not indexable")
}

func expectRun(
	t *testing.T,
	input string,
	opts *testopts,
	expected interface{},
) {
	if opts == nil {
		opts = Opts()
	}

	symbols := opts.symbols
	modules := opts.modules
	maxAllocs := opts.maxAllocs

	expectedObj := toObject(expected)

	if symbols == nil {
		symbols = make(map[string]tengo.Object)
	}
	symbols[testOut] = objectZeroCopy(expectedObj)

	// first pass: run the code normally
	{
		// parse
		file := parse(t, input)
		if file == nil {
			return
		}

		// compiler/VM
		res, trace, err := traceCompileRun(file, symbols, modules, maxAllocs)
		require.NoError(t, err, "\n"+strings.Join(trace, "\n"))
=== VM 3900 ===
}

func parse(t *testing.T, input string) *parser.File {
	testFileSet := parser.NewFileSet()
	testFile := testFileSet.AddFile("test", -1, len(input))

	p := parser.NewParser(testFile, []byte(input), nil)
	file, err := p.ParseFile()
	require.NoError(t, err)
	return file
}

func errorObject(v interface{}) *tengo.Error {
	return &tengo.Error{Value: toObject(v)}
}

func TestArray(t *testing.T) {
	expectRun(t, `out = [1, 2 * 2, 3 + 3]`, nil, ARR{1, 4, 6})

	// array copy-by-reference
	expectRun(t, `a1 := [1, 2, 3]; a2 := a1; a1[0] = 5; out = a2`,
		nil, ARR{5, 2, 3})
	expectRun(t, `func () { a1 := [1, 2, 3]; a2 := a1; a1[0] = 5; out = a2 }()`,
		nil, ARR{5, 2, 3})

	// array index set
	expectError(t, `a1 := [1, 2, 3]; a1[3] = 5`,
		nil, "index out of bounds")

	// index operator
	arr := ARR{1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6}
	arrStr := `[1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6]`
	arrLen := 6
	for idx := 0; idx < arrLen; idx++ {
		expectRun(t, fmt.Sprintf("out = %s[%d]", arrStr, idx),
			nil, arr[idx])
		expectRun(t, fmt.Sprintf("out = %s[0 + %d]", arrStr, idx),
			nil, arr[idx])
		expectRun(t, fmt.Sprintf("out = %s[1 + %d - 1]", arrStr, idx),
			nil, arr[idx])
		expectRun(t, fmt.Sprintf("idx := %d; out = %s[idx]", idx, arrStr),
			nil, arr[idx])
	}

	expectRun(t, fmt.Sprintf("%s[%d]", arrStr, -1),
		nil, tengo.UndefinedValue)
	expectRun(t, fmt.Sprintf("%s[%d]", arrStr, arrLen),
		nil, tengo.UndefinedValue)

	// slice operator

func (p *Parser) parseOperand() Expr {
	if p.trace {
		defer untracep(tracep(p, "Operand"))
	}

	switch p.token {
	case token.Ident:
		return p.parseIdent()
	case token.Int:
		v, err := strconv.ParseInt(p.tokenLit, 0, 64)
		if err == strconv.ErrRange {
			p.error(p.pos, "number out of range")
		} else if err != nil {
			p.error(p.pos, "invalid integer")
		}
		x := &IntLit{
			Value:    v,
			ValuePos: p.pos,
			Literal:  p.tokenLit,
		}
		p.next()
		return x

	case token.Float:
		v, err := strconv.ParseFloat(p.tokenLit, 64)
		if err == strconv.ErrRange {
			p.error(p.pos, "number out of range")
		} else if err != nil {
			p.error(p.pos, "invalid float")
		}
		x := &FloatLit{
			Value:    v,
			ValuePos: p.pos,
			Literal:  p.tokenLit,
		}
		p.next()
		return x
	case token.Char:
		return p.parseCharLit()
	case token.String:
		v, _ := strconv.Unquote(p.tokenLit)
		x := &StringLit{
			Value:    v,
			ValuePos: p.pos,
			Literal:  p.tokenLit,
		}
		p.next()
		return x
	case token.True:
		x := &BoolLit{
			Value:    true,
			ValuePos: p.pos,
			Literal:  p.tokenLit,
		}
		p.next()
		return x
	case token.False:
		x := &BoolLit{
			Value:    false,
			ValuePos: p.pos,
			Literal:  p.tokenLit,
		}
		p.next()
		return x
	case token.Undefined:
		x := &UndefinedLit{TokenPos: p.pos}
		p.next()
		return x
	case token.Import:
		return p.parseImportExpr()
	case token.LParen:
		lparen := p.pos
		p.next()
		p.exprLevel++
		x := p.parseExpr()
		p.exprLevel--
		rparen := p.expect(token.RParen)
		return &ParenExpr{
			LParen: lparen,
			Expr:   x,
			RParen: rparen,
		}
	case token.LBrack: // array literal
		return p.parseArrayLit()
	case token.LBrace: // map literal
		return p.parseMapLit()
	case token.Func: // function literal
		return p.parseFuncLit()

func (p *Parser) parseUnaryExpr() Expr {
	if p.trace {
		defer untracep(tracep(p, "UnaryExpression"))
	}

	switch p.token {
	case token.Add, token.Sub, token.Not, token.Xor:
		pos, op := p.pos, p.token
		p.next()
		x := p.parseUnaryExpr()
		return &UnaryExpr{
			Token:    op,
			TokenPos: pos,
			Expr:     x,
		}
	}
	return p.parsePrimaryExpr()
}

func (p *Parser) parsePrimaryExpr() Expr {
	if p.trace {
		defer untracep(tracep(p, "PrimaryExpression"))
	}

	x := p.parseOperand()

L:
	for {
		switch p.token {
		case token.Period:
			p.next()

			switch p.token {
			case token.Ident:
				x = p.parseSelector(x)
			default:
				pos := p.pos
				p.errorExpected(pos, "selector")
				p.advance(stmtStart)
				return &BadExpr{From: pos, To: p.pos}
			}
		case token.LBrack:
			x = p.parseIndexOrSlice(x)
		case token.LParen:
			x = p.parseCall(x)
		default:
			break L
		}
	}
	return x
}

func (p *Parser) parseCall(x Expr) *CallExpr {
	if p.trace {
		defer untracep(tracep(p, "Call"))
	}

	lparen := p.expect(token.LParen)
	p.exprLevel++

	var list []Expr
	var ellipsis Pos
	for p.token != token.RParen && p.token != token.EOF && !ellipsis.IsValid() {
		list = append(list, p.parseExpr())
		if p.token == token.Ellipsis {
			ellipsis = p.pos
			p.next()
		}
		if !p.expectComma(token.RParen, "call argument") {
			break
		}
	}

	p.exprLevel--
	rparen := p.expect(token.RParen)
	return &CallExpr{
		Func:     x,
		LParen:   lparen,
		RParen:   rparen,
		Ellipsis: ellipsis,
		Args:     list,
	}
}

func (p *Parser) expectComma(closing token.Token, want string) bool {
	if p.token == token.Comma {
		p.next()

		if p.token == closing {
			p.errorExpected(p.pos, want)
			return false
		}
		return true
	}

	if p.token == token.Semicolon && p.tokenLit == "\n" {
		p.next()
	}
	return false
}

func (p *Parser) parseIndexOrSlice(x Expr) Expr {
	if p.trace {
		defer untracep(tracep(p, "IndexOrSlice"))
	}

	lbrack := p.expect(token.LBrack)
	p.exprLevel++

	var index [2]Expr
	if p.token != token.Colon {
		index[0] = p.parseExpr()
	}
	numColons := 0
	if p.token == token.Colon {
		numColons++
		p.next()

		if p.token != token.RBrack && p.token != token.EOF {
			index[1] = p.parseExpr()
		}
	}

	p.exprLevel--
	rbrack := p.expect(token.RBrack)

	if numColons > 0 {
		// slice expression
		return &SliceExpr{
			Expr:   x,
			LBrack: lbrack,
			RBrack: rbrack,
			Low:    index[0],
			High:   index[1],
		}
	}
	return &IndexExpr{
		Expr:   x,
		LBrack: lbrack,
		RBrack: rbrack,
		Index:  index[0],
	}
}

func (p *Parser) parseSelector(x Expr) Expr {

STEP 81 SOURCE agent
REASONING_CONTENT
Line 135 is `expectRun(t, fmt.Sprintf("%s[%d]", arrStr, -1), ...)` — input is `[1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6][-1]` as a standalone expression statement. This is
where our `parseExprPattern` kicks in! The statement starts with `[`, so `parseExprPattern` parses it as an ArrayPattern, then sees token `[` (the
index `[`), which is not `:=`/`=`, so it calls `convertPatternToLiteral(pat)` and then `parseBinaryExprTail(lit, ...)`.

But `convertPatternToLiteral` returns an ArrayLit. Then `parseBinaryExprTail` sees token `[` — but `[` is not a binary operator (precedence 0), so it
returns the ArrayLit. Then `parseSimpleStmt` sees token `[` (the index operator), which is not in its switch -> `p.expectSemi()` fails with "expected
';', found '['".

The problem: after converting to literal, we need to continue parsing postfix operations (index, call, selector), not just binary operators. The
postfix operations are handled in `parsePrimaryExpr` after `parseOperand`. When we parse a pattern and then convert to literal, we skipped the postfix
loop.

Fix: After converting pattern to literal in `parseExprPattern`, continue with `parsePrimaryExprTail(lit)` (the postfix loop). Let me refactor
`parsePrimaryExpr` into a tail too, or simply call a helper that continues the postfix loop and then binary tail.

Let me refactor:
```go
func (p *Parser) parsePrimaryExpr() Expr {
    x := p.parseOperand()
    return p.parsePrimaryExprTail(x)
}

func (p *Parser) parsePrimaryExprTail(x Expr) Expr {
L:
    for {
        switch p.token {
        case token.Period: ...
        case token.LBrack: x = p.parseIndexOrSlice(x)
        case token.LParen: x = p.parseCall(x)
        default: break L
        }
    }
    return x
}
```

Then in `parseExprPattern`:
```go
lit := p.convertPatternToLiteral(pat)
if lit != nil {
    lit = p.parsePrimaryExprTail(lit)
    return p.parseBinaryExprTail(lit, token.LowestPrec+1)
}
```

Let me refactor.
MESSAGE

command:
cd /app && python3 - <<'PY'
p='parser/parser.go'
s=open(p).read()
old='''func (p *Parser) parsePrimaryExpr() Expr {
	if p.trace {
		defer untracep(tracep(p, "PrimaryExpression"))
	}

	x := p.parseOperand()

L:
	for {
		switch p.token {
		case token.Period:
			p.next()

			switch p.token {
			case token.Ident:
				x = p.parseSelector(x)
			default:
				pos := p.pos
				p.errorExpected(pos, "selector")
				p.advance(stmtStart)
				return &BadExpr{From: pos, To: p.pos}
			}
		case token.LBrack:
			x = p.parseIndexOrSlice(x)
		case token.LParen:
			x = p.parseCall(x)
		default:
			break L
		}
	}
	return x
}'''
new='''func (p *Parser) parsePrimaryExpr() Expr {
	if p.trace {
		defer untracep(tracep(p, "PrimaryExpression"))
	}

	x := p.parseOperand()
	return p.parsePrimaryExprTail(x)
}

func (p *Parser) parsePrimaryExprTail(x Expr) Expr {
L:
	for {
		switch p.token {
		case token.Period:
			p.next()

			switch p.token {
			case token.Ident:
				x = p.parseSelector(x)
			default:
				pos := p.pos
				p.errorExpected(pos, "selector")
				p.advance(stmtStart)
				return &BadExpr{From: pos, To: p.pos}
			}
		case token.LBrack:
			x = p.parseIndexOrSlice(x)
		case token.LParen:
			x = p.parseCall(x)
		default:
			break L
		}
	}
	return x
}'''

s=s.replace(old,new,1)

old='''		pat := p.parsePattern()
		if p.token != token.Assign && p.token != token.Define {
			if lit := p.convertPatternToLiteral(pat); lit != nil {
				return p.parseBinaryExprTail(lit, token.LowestPrec+1)
			}
		}
		return pat'''
new='''		pat := p.parsePattern()
		if p.token != token.Assign && p.token != token.Define {
			if lit := p.convertPatternToLiteral(pat); lit != nil {
				lit = p.parsePrimaryExprTail(lit)
				return p.parseBinaryExprTail(lit, token.LowestPrec+1)
			}
		}
		return pat'''
assert old in s
s=s.replace(old,new,1)
open(p,'w').write(s)
print("ok")

func expectParse(t *testing.T, input string, fn expectedFn) {
	testFileSet := NewFileSet()
	testFile := testFileSet.AddFile("test", -1, len(input))

	var ok bool
	defer func() {
		if !ok {
			// print trace
			tr := &parseTracer{}
			p := NewParser(testFile, []byte(input), tr)
			actual, _ := p.ParseFile()
			if actual != nil {
				t.Logf("Parsed:\n%s", actual.String())
			}
			t.Logf("Trace:\n%s", strings.Join(tr.out, ""))
		}
	}()

	p := NewParser(testFile, []byte(input), nil)
	actual, err := p.ParseFile()
	require.NoError(t, err)

	expected := fn(func(line, column int) Pos {
		return Pos(int(testFile.LineStart(line)) + (column - 1))
	})
	require.Equal(t, len(expected), len(actual.Stmts))

	for i := 0; i < len(expected); i++ {
		equalStmt(t, expected[i], actual.Stmts[i])
	}

	ok = true
}

func expectParseError(t *testing.T, input string) {
	testFileSet := NewFileSet()
	testFile := testFileSet.AddFile("test", -1, len(input))

	var ok bool

	}

func selectorExpr(x, sel Expr) *SelectorExpr {
	return &SelectorExpr{Expr: x, Sel: sel}
}

func equalStmt(t *testing.T, expected, actual Stmt) {
	if expected == nil || reflect.ValueOf(expected).IsNil() {
		require.Nil(t, actual, "expected nil, but got not nil")
		return
	}
	require.NotNil(t, actual, "expected not nil, but got nil")
	require.IsType(t, expected, actual)

	switch expected := expected.(type) {
	case *ExprStmt:
		equalExpr(t, expected.Expr, actual.(*ExprStmt).Expr)
	case *EmptyStmt:
		require.Equal(t, expected.Implicit,
			actual.(*EmptyStmt).Implicit)
		require.Equal(t, expected.Semicolon,
			actual.(*EmptyStmt).Semicolon)
	case *BlockStmt:
		require.Equal(t, expected.LBrace,
			actual.(*BlockStmt).LBrace)
		equalExpr(t, expected.Expr,
			actual.(*ParenExpr).Expr)
		require.Equal(t, expected.LParen,
			actual.(*ParenExpr).LParen)
		require.Equal(t, expected.RParen,
			actual.(*ParenExpr).RParen)
	case *IndexExpr:
		equalExpr(t, expected.Expr,
			actual.(*IndexExpr).Expr)
		equalExpr(t, expected.Index,
			actual.(*IndexExpr).Index)

func equalMapElements(
	t *testing.T,
	expected, actual []*MapElementLit,
) {
	require.Equal(t, len(expected), len(actual))
	for i := 0; i < len(expected); i++ {
		require.Equal(t, expected[i].Key, actual[i].Key)
		require.Equal(t, expected[i].KeyPos, actual[i].KeyPos)
		require.Equal(t, expected[i].ColonPos, actual[i].ColonPos)
		equalExpr(t, expected[i].Value, actual[i].Value)
	}
}

func parseSource(
	filename string,
	src []byte,
	trace io.Writer,
) (res *File, err error) {
	fileSet := NewFileSet()
	file := fileSet.AddFile(filename, -1, len(src))

	p := NewParser(file, src, trace)
	return p.ParseFile()
}

			})'''
assert old in s

STEP 95 SOURCE agent
REASONING_CONTENT
Panic at vm.go:257 — likely `v.stack[v.sp]` out of range, maybe in OpJumpIfUndefined or the jump logic. The panic occurred after printing first
several lines; last output was "150" (the default case) then panic on `[a4, b4 = 50] := [1, 2]`? Wait output:
- `12` from d1? No this is d2. Actually output lines:
1. `12` — first println `x,z` -> x=1, z=2 -> "12"
2. `1020` — a,b = 10,20
3. `123` — a2,b2,c2 = 1,2,3
4. `150` — a3,b3=50 -> a3=1,b3=50
5. panic — on `[a4, b4 = 50] := [1, 2]` where b4 should be 2 (not default).

The panic suggests our default logic has a bug when the value is NOT undefined. Let's look at the generated code. The issue may be in
`compileDefaultPattern`: after `OpJumpIfUndefined` (no jump) the value remains on stack, then `OpJump` jumps to end, but the jump target computation
might be wrong. Let me trace.

Sequence for `b4 = 50` when value is 2:
```
... push value (2)
JMPUNDEF L1   ; sp--, 2 not undefined, no jump; sp points to 2 (value stays at stack[sp])
JMP L2        ; jump to L2
L1:
  push 50
L2:
  DEFL b
```
After JMPUNDEF: sp was decremented, so value 2 is at stack[sp]. Then JMP L2 jumps to L2 (which is after the default compile). At L2, the stack top is
2. DEFL b pops it. Good.

But wait: when the value IS undefined:
```
push undefined
JMPUNDEF L1   ; sp--, undefined, jump to L1
L1:
  push 50
L2:
  DEFL b
```
At L1, undefined was popped (sp points past it). Then push 50. DEFL b pops 50. Good.

So why panic? Let me check the actual emitted code. Maybe `OpJumpIfUndefined`'s position reading is wrong because I set `v.ip += 4` then read
`v.curInsts[v.ip-1]` etc. Let's compare with OpJumpFalsy:
```
case parser.OpJumpFalsy:
    v.ip += 4
    v.sp--
    if v.stack[v.sp].IsFalsy() {
        pos := int(v.curInsts[v.ip]) | int(v.curInsts[v.ip-1])<<8 | ...
        v.ip = pos - 1
    }
```
That reads bytes at v.ip-1, v.ip-2, v.ip-3 (since v.ip was advanced by 4, v.ip-1 is the last operand byte). My OpJumpIfUndefined copied this. Good.

Hmm, let me get a stack trace to see the exact instruction. Let me run with trace by modifying or using a small Go program that compiles with trace.
Easier: use the REPL? No. Let's write a tiny Go program to compile and print instructions. Or use `go test` with a new test. Let me create a quick
debug program.

package main

import (
	"bytes"
	"fmt"
	"github.com/d5/tengo/v2"
	"github.com/d5/tengo/v2/parser"
)

func main() {
	src := `[a, b = 50] := [1, 2]`
	fileSet := parser.NewFileSet()
	file := fileSet.AddFile("test", -1, len(src))
	p := parser.NewParser(file, []byte(src), nil)
	f, err := p.ParseFile()
	if err != nil { panic(err) }
	c := tengo.NewCompiler(file, nil, nil, nil, nil)
	if err := c.Compile(f); err != nil { panic(err) }
	bc := c.Bytecode()

48:func (b *Bytecode) FormatInstructions() []string {
package tengo

import (
	"encoding/gob"
	"fmt"
	"io"
	"reflect"

	"github.com/d5/tengo/v2/parser"
)

// Bytecode is a compiled instructions and constants.
type Bytecode struct {
	FileSet      *parser.SourceFileSet
	MainFunction *CompiledFunction
	Constants    []Object
}

// Size of the bytecode in bytes
// (as much as we can calculate it without reflection and black magic)
func (b *Bytecode) Size() int64 {
	return b.MainFunction.Size() + b.FileSet.Size() + int64(len(b.Constants))
}

// Encode writes Bytecode data to the writer.
func (b *Bytecode) Encode(w io.Writer) error {
	enc := gob.NewEncoder(w)
	if err := enc.Encode(b.FileSet); err != nil {
		return err
	}
	if err := enc.Encode(b.MainFunction); err != nil {
		return err
	}
	return enc.Encode(b.Constants)
}

// CountObjects returns the number of objects found in Constants.
func (b *Bytecode) CountObjects() int {
	n := 0
	for _, c := range b.Constants {
		n += CountObjects(c)
	}
	return n
}

// FormatInstructions returns human readable string representations of
// compiled instructions.
func (b *Bytecode) FormatInstructions() []string {
	return FormatInstructions(b.MainFunction.Instructions, 0)
}

// FormatConstants returns human readable string representations of
// compiled constants.
func (b *Bytecode) FormatConstants() (output []string) {
	for cidx, cn := range b.Constants {
		switch cn := cn.(type) {
		case *CompiledFunction:
			output = append(output, fmt.Sprintf(
				"[% 3d] (Compiled Function|%p)", cidx, &cn))
			for _, l := range FormatInstructions(cn.Instructions, 0) {
				output = append(output, fmt.Sprintf("     %s", l))
			}
		default:
			output = append(output, fmt.Sprintf("[% 3d] %s (%s|%p)",
				cidx, cn, reflect.TypeOf(cn).Elem().Name(), &cn))
		}
	}
	return
}

// Decode reads Bytecode data from the reader.
func (b *Bytecode) Decode(r io.Reader, modules *ModuleMap) error {
	if modules == nil {
		modules = NewModuleMap()
	}

	dec := gob.NewDecoder(r)
	if err := dec.Decode(&b.FileSet); err != nil {
		return err
	}

STEP 100 SOURCE agent
REASONING_CONTENT
The panic is at `v.globals[globalIndex] = v.stack[v.sp]` — index out of range [-1] means `v.sp` became -1. So the stack underflowed. This happens
because after `JMPUNDEF` (no jump), the value should remain on the stack. But maybe OpJumpIfUndefined's semantics conflict with `v.sp--`: when no
jump, `v.sp--` leaves the value at index `v.sp` and sp now points to that value — correct, the value is at top. Then `JMP` doesn't affect sp. Then
`SETG` does `v.sp--` (now -1 relative to expected?) Wait:

Let's trace stack for `[a, b = 50] := [1, 2]` at top level (global):
- CONST 0, CONST 1, ARR 2 -> stack: [arr], sp=1
- SETG 0: sp-- -> sp=0, globals[0]=arr. Stack empty.
- GETG 0: push arr -> sp=1
- CONST 2: push 0 -> sp=2
- INDEX: pops 2, pushes result (1) -> sp=1
- SETG 1 (a): sp-- -> sp=0. Good.
- GETG 0: sp=1
- CONST 3: push 1 -> sp=2
- INDEX: sp=1 (value 2)
- JMPUNDEF 39: v.ip += 4, v.sp-- -> sp=0, stack[0]=2, not undefined -> no jump.
- JMP 42: jumps, sp=0.
- CONST 4: push 50 -> sp=1
- SETG 2: sp-- -> sp=0. Good.

That should work. But the panic says sp = -1 at SETG. That means before SETG, sp was 0 and stack[0] was empty? Wait, SETG does `v.sp--` then reads
`v.stack[v.sp]`. If sp was 0, then v.sp-- -> -1, and stack[-1] panic. So before SETG, sp=0 (empty stack) — meaning the value was consumed somewhere.

Where could the value be consumed? Let me re-examine JMPUNDEF when no jump:
```
case parser.OpJumpIfUndefined:
    v.ip += 4
    v.sp--
    if _, isUndefined := v.stack[v.sp].(*Undefined); isUndefined { ... }
```
After this, sp=0, and stack[0]=2. The value is at index 0. That's fine; "top of stack" is index 0 and sp=1 normally means one element. Wait, in this
VM, sp points to the next free slot (elements at 0..sp-1). When sp=0, stack is empty; but we have stack[0]=2 leftover. That's inconsistent: after
JMPUNDEF, sp=0 but there IS an element at index 0. So the top element is at index `v.sp` (0), not `v.sp-1`! That's the bug in the JMPF pattern?

Let me re-read OpJumpFalsy:
```
case parser.OpJumpFalsy:
    v.ip += 4
    v.sp--
    if v.stack[v.sp].IsFalsy() { ... }
```
Before OpJumpFalsy, sp=1 (one value at index 0). After sp--, sp=0. It reads stack[0]. If not falsy, it does nothing — sp=0, and the value at stack[0]
is considered "on the stack" even though sp=0. But wait, that can't be right for normal usage. Let me look at how OpJumpFalsy is used in logical
operators. In `compileLogical`:
```
compile LHS  ; sp=1
OpAndJump  ; ...
```
OpAndJump does NOT decrement sp when the value is truthy and continues; it leaves the value? Actually:
```
case parser.OpAndJump:
    v.ip += 4
    if v.stack[v.sp-1].IsFalsy() { jump } else { v.sp-- }
```
So OpAndJump pops on truthy. OpJumpFalsy is used for if conditions where the value is consumed either way (jump or not jump). In `if`, after
OpJumpFalsy, if condition is truthy, the value remains at stack[sp] with sp decremented... wait no. Let's trace `if x { }`:
- compile cond -> sp=1
- OpJumpFalsy: v.sp-- -> sp=0, reads stack[0]. If truthy, does NOT jump, sp=0. Then compiles body. But body expects sp=0 (empty). So the condition
value was effectively consumed! Because sp=0 means empty, even though stack[0] has leftover garbage. So OpJumpFalsy ALWAYS pops the value (decrements
sp), and on jump it also discards. The "value remains" I thought is wrong — after `sp--`, the stack is considered empty (sp points to the slot, but
that slot is now considered "below sp", i.e., not part of the stack).

So my JMPUNDEF/OpJump usage is wrong: OpJumpFalsy-style instruction always consumes the value (via sp--) regardless of branch. For the default
pattern, on the not-undefined branch we need the value to REMAIN on the stack. So we can't use the JMPF-style instruction that decrements sp
unconditionally.

Options:
1. Add a new opcode `OpJumpIfUndefined` that does NOT decrement sp when not undefined (keeps value), and pops it when undefined. This is what I
intended but implemented wrong.
2. Or use a temp variable approach: store value, test, reload.

Let me fix OpJumpIfUndefined: only decrement when jumping (undefined). When not undefined, keep sp unchanged.

```go
case parser.OpJumpIfUndefined:
    v.ip += 4
    if _, isUndefined := v.stack[v.sp-1].(*Undefined); isUndefined {
        v.sp--
        pos := ...
        v.ip = pos - 1
    }
```

This matches the semantics I need: on undefined, pop and jump; on not undefined, leave value on stack.

Let me fix vm.go.

1. `[r1, ...rest] := [1]` output was `1[]`? Wait output lines:
   - line1 `1` -> a=1,b=undefined (println prints "1" with undefined?)
   - line2 `1` -> x=1,y=undefined
   - line3 blank -> [] := [] (prints nothing? Actually `fmt.println()` with no args -> blank line)
   - line4 `1[]` -> c=1, then r1=1, rest=[] printed together "1[]" — wait no. Let me map: after blank line (line3 from `fmt.println()`), line4 should
be c=1. Then line5 should be r1, rest. But there's only "1[]" as line 4 in output. Hmm, the output lines:
   ```
   1
   1

   1[]
   Runtime Error...
   ```
   So line1: a=1 (b undefined prints as empty?) Actually `fmt.println(a,b)` with b undefined prints "1<undefined>"? Let me check: output "1" means
maybe b prints empty. Whatever.

   The runtime error is `invalid slice index: 1 > 0` at line 12: `[r2, ...rest2] := []` — slicing empty array from index 1 gives invalid slice index 1
> 0 (numElements=0, low=1, high=0, and VM checks `if lowIdx > highIdx`). This is a bug: rest when start index >= length should produce empty array,
not error.

In VM's OpSliceIndex for Array:
```go
if lowIdx > highIdx {
    v.err = fmt.Errorf("invalid slice index: %d > %d", lowIdx, highIdx)
    return
}
```
So slicing `arr[1:]` on empty array errors. We need to handle rest specially: if start index >= len, bind empty array.

Options:
- Compile rest differently: check length. But there's no length opcode.
- Add a new opcode for rest collection that handles out-of-range gracefully, e.g., `OpRestSlice` that does `array[idx:]` clamping.
- Or in compiler, guard the slice: if source is an array of length <= idx, set empty array. But no length opcode.

Let me add a new opcode `OpRest` / `OpRestSlice`:
```
; stack: [array, startIndex]
OpRest
; pops start, array; pushes new array array[start:] (empty if start >= len)
```
This handles Array and ImmutableArray. What if source is undefined or not an array? Runtime error "not indexable" or similar.

Actually maybe simpler: compile rest as:
```
GET source
CONST idx
; check if idx >= len -> need len
```
No len opcode. New opcode is cleanest.

Let me add `OpRest` with no operands (or 1?). Stack: [array, startInt]. Implementation:
```go
case parser.OpRest:
    start := v.stack[v.sp-1]
    left := v.stack[v.sp-2]
    v.sp -= 2
    startIdx, ok := start.(*Int)  // we always push Int
    if !ok { err }
    switch left := left.(type) {
    case *Array:
        num := int64(len(left.Value))
        if startIdx.Value > num { startIdx.Value = num }
        if startIdx.Value < 0 { startIdx.Value = 0 }  // shouldn't happen
        res := &Array{Value: left.Value[startIdx.Value:]}
    case *ImmutableArray: similar
    default:
        v.err = fmt.Errorf("not indexable: %s", left.TypeName())
    }
```
Wait, but `left.Value[startIdx.Value:]` with startIdx.Value as int64 — Go slice index must be int. Convert.

Actually, for `Array`, `left.Value` is `[]Object`. `int(startIdx.Value)`. Clamp to [0, len].

Let me add this opcode.

Alternatively, I could make OpSliceIndex handle `low > high` by clamping instead of erroring. But that would change existing semantics for `arr[5:1]`
(currently errors; tests may rely on that). Let me check existing tests for slice invalid.

			}

				v.err = fmt.Errorf("not indexable: %s", left.TypeName())

		case parser.OpRest:
			start := v.stack[v.sp-1]
			left := v.stack[v.sp-2]
			v.sp -= 2

			startIdx, ok := start.(*Int)
			if !ok {
				v.err = fmt.Errorf("invalid rest index type: %s",

				return

				if startIdx.Value > numElements {

				}
				if startIdx.Value < 0 {
					startIdx.Value = 0
				}

					Value: left.Value[int(startIdx.Value):],
				}

					return

			case *ImmutableArray:
